The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this usefulA raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.4.1identityStatusAlready covered, but worth repeating:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknown4.2responsibilityProfileWhat is this thingfor?JSON{"responsibilityProfile": {"primaryFunction":"semantic_memory","secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage","identity_canonicalization"],"boundedContext":"architecture_memory","antiResponsibilities": ["operational task queue","ephemeral working set"]}}SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:semantic_memoryoperational_memoryruntime_orchestrationservice_brokeringsemantic_executiondocument_ingestionprojection_generationgovernancecapability_augmentationui_interactionintegration_surfaceThis lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”4.3topologyRoleThis is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.SourcesYou said:Turn this into a concrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draft for Nexus.TopologyChatGPT said:Good — this is exactly the point where it should stop being conceptual and become something a compiler or generator can actually eat.Below is aconcrete hybrid TypeSpec + JSON-LD framingforNexus.Topology. I’m going to structure it so:TypeSpec = authoritative schema contract (generation-friendly)JSON-LD context = semantic graph binding (self-describing + queryable)Design stays aligned with your CIR/CER/CGEL/WRP world1. deferreddecidedBy:string;decidedAt:utcDateTime;resultingAssertion?:string;notes?:string;}}3. JSON-LD sample instance (LOSM example)This shows how the schema actuallybehavesas a graph.JSON{"@context":"nexus-topology.context.jsonld","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityId":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","identityStatus":"optional","topologyRole":"semantic_execution_engine","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"description":"Optional semantic execution engine used for graph-based reasoning and process orchestration.","implementedBy": ["repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-host"],"runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","external":true}}4. What this buys you immediatelyThis schema is not just representation — it gives youthree hard capabilities:1. Self-inventoryNexus can answer:what existswhere it liveswhat kind of thing it is2. Self-interpretationBecause of:identityStatusTopologyAssertionTopologyRoleNexus can say:“LOSM is optional”“PEB is constitutive”“OpenCode is external harness state”3. Self-topology explanationBecause of edges + assertions:“what uses what”“why it exists”“what would break if removed”5. The key design win (this is the important part)This schema cleanly separates:Structure (truth)entitiesedgesMeaning (interpretation)assertionsidentityStatustopologyRoleEvidence (epistemology)why we believe anything at allThat separation is what makes:“Nexus can tell us what it is and how it thinks about itself”actually work instead of becoming a static diagram.If you want the next step, the natural continuation is:a query layer for this model(e.g. “show all optional capability modules and what they depend on” or “explain Nexus core identity boundary”), which is where it starts becoming a real introspection engine instead of just a schema.

immediate target than trying to freeze the Assistance boundary too early.What you want first isNexus self-description.Not a documentaboutNexus that you keep hand-updating, but a system that can does not consider part of its core identity”3.Responsibility map“What each component is for”4.Dependency graph“Who calls whom / who depends on whom / what data crosses the boundary”5.Confidence / provenance“How Nexus knows this, and how sure it is”6.Known topology ambiguities“LOSM may be embedded vs externalized”, “OpenCode is harness-only”, etc.That’s the artifact that will actually let you cross the bridge cleanly later.I think this implies a pretty specific sequencePhase 1 — Make Nexus observable to itselfBefore deciding what Assistance keeps, get Nexus to enumerate:componentsroles of componentscontracts / interfacesdependenciesexecution paths“is/uses” distinctionsThis is basically:self-inventory + self-topology + self-interpretationPhase 2 — Let Nexus classify its own partsOnce it can enumerate, it can start labeling things like:coreadapterexternal tooloptional capabilitylegacy artifactexperimental subsystemThis is where LOSM starts getting identified as:“used by Nexus” rather than “part of Nexus”but you letNexus tell you that, rather than imposing it too early.Phase 3 — Use that self-description as Assistance inputThen the Assistance bridge becomes grounded in evidence:“Nexus says these are core”“Nexus says these are optional”“these interfaces are stable enough to preserve”“these modules are really external consumers/providers”That’s much better than deciding by intuition alone.What Nexus needs in order to tell you thisFor Nexus to answer “what do I contain / what do I use / what do I think my topology is?”, it needs at least three layers of representation:1.Inventory layerA registry of components/artifacts/modules/services/repos/processes.Questions it should answer:what exists?where does it live?what type of thing is it?2.Relationship layerLinks like:containsdepends_oncallsimplementsgeneratespersists_toreads_fromoptional_forconsumes_serviceprovides_capabilityQuestions it should answer:what uses LOSM?what depends on PGV?is Vision mounted or constitutive?3.Interpretation layerA higher-level “what is this for?” view.Questions it should answer:is this identity-defining or auxiliary?is this runtime, storage, governance, cognition, Topology Self-Model schema for NexusChatGPT said:Thought for 6sAbsolutely. I’d sketch it as aJSON-LD / graph-native topology self-modelthat lives on thePEB/KG side as meaning + identity + provenance, withNebula as the operational/work projection. In other words:Nexus should describe itself semantically in the PEB, and Nebula should carry the reviewable operational slices, scans, and snapshots that feed or project from it.What follows isnot“final Assistance ontology.” It’s theintermediate Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM): the minimum model Nexus needs in order to answer:What do I contain?What do I use?What is each thing for?What is core vs optional vs external?What do I believe my topology is right now?How do I know that, and how confident am I?Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM) — Schema Sketch v0.10. Design goalsThe TSM should let Nexus representboth structure and interpretation.It needs to capture:Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems existRelationships— how they depend on or use each otherInterpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curationTemporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what roleThat means the model needsthree layers:Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placementsInterpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classificationEvidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context1. Top-level object modelI’d center it on aTopologySnapshotthat contains a graph ofTopologyEntities,TopologyEdges, andTopologyAssertions.Primary classesA.TopologySnapshotA frozen view of “what Nexus believes its topology is” at a moment in time.B.TopologyEntityA node in the topology graph:repomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolMCPschema/IRdocument corpusexternal systemcapability moduleharness concernC.TopologyEdgeA typed relationship between two entities:containsdepends_onusesinvokeshostspersists_toreads_fromwrites_toprojects_toderives_fromsupersedesbridges_tooptional_forrequired_forgoverned_byexposed_viaD.TopologyAssertionAn interpretable claim about an entity or relationship:“LOSM is optional for Nexus”“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“OpenCode role state is a harness concern, not a project role”“CGEL is a portable semantic contract likely to survive the bridge”E.TopologyEvidenceA provenance-bearing observation supporting an assertion:repo scan resultimport graphdocs passagechange log eventsteward classificationtranscript quotemanual declaration2. Core schema objects2.1TopologySnapshotThis is the anchor record for a reportable state of the world.JSON{"@type":"TopologySnapshot","snapshotId":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","system":"Nexus","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","scope": {"repos": ["nexus","conduit","tackle","losm-*"],"branches": ["main","feature/..."],"workRequests": ["WR-123","WR-124"],"harvestWindow": {"from":"2026-06-20T00:00:00Z","to":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}},"topologyStateVector": {"entityCount":87,"edgeCount":241,"assertionCount":64,"confidenceScore":0.78},"defaultProjection": {"inventoryView":"view://topology/inventory/core","dependencyView":"view://topology/dependencies/runtime","identityView":"view://topology/identity-vs-optional"},"containsEntities": ["entity:peb","entity:nebula","entity:losm","entity:wrp"],"containsEdges": ["edge:1","edge:2","edge:3"],"containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional","assertion:peb-canonical"],"derivedFrom": ["harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24","harvest:docs-2026-06-24","gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"]}Why this mattersThis gives you thesnapshot-able state vectoryou were talking about around levels / frozen graph state / pointer projection. The snapshot is the thing Nebula can review, diff, and surface; the semantic identity of the nodes/edges/assertions belongs on the PEB/KG side.2.2TopologyEntityA general node type with subtype classification.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEntity","@id":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","subtype":"semantic_execution_engine","lifecycleState":"active","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"identityStatus":"optional","runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","hostedBy":null,"consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]},"sourceOfTruth": {"semantic":"PEB","operational":"Nebula"},"descriptions": {"summary":"Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."},"tags": ["bridge-candidate","optional-upgrade","extractable"],"ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],"implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-host"]}Key fieldsentityKindOne of:systemrepomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolmcpschemairmemory_substratecapability_moduleharness_featuredocument_corpusexternal_systemui_surfacedatabaseprojectionsnapshotidentityStatusThis is critical. It should answer “is this part of what Nexusis?”Suggested enum:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknownExamples:PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrateLOSM / Vision→optionalOpenCode mode state→harness_feature+external_dependencyGitHub / Ollama / OpenCode itself→external_dependency2.3TopologyEdgeA typed relationship with optional runtime / contract metadata.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEdge","@id":"edge:nexus-uses-losm","from":"entity:nexus-runtime","to":"entity:losm","relationshipType":"uses","cardinality":"0..1","strength":"optional","directionality":"outbound","semanticMeaning":"Nexus can invoke LOSM as a semantic execution capability.","activationConditions": ["workload requires semantic execution / graph processing"],"evidenceRefs": ["evidence:losm-integration-doc","evidence:repo-scan-losm-client"]}Relationship types I’d define up frontStructuralcontainspart_ofhostsimplemented_byDependency / executiondepends_onusesinvokesreads_fromwrites_topersists_toqueriesroutes_throughbootstrapsSemantic / representationalprojects_toderived_fromtranslated_tomirrorssupersedesbridges_toGovernance / identitygoverned_bycanonicalized_byclassified_asoptional_forrequired_forTopology-specificexposed_viamounted_intoconsumed_byshares_contract_with2.4TopologyAssertionThis is where the self-model stops being just a code graph and starts becomingwhat Nexus thinks about itself.JSON{"@type":"TopologyAssertion","@id":"assertion:losm-optional","subject":"entity:losm","predicate":"identityStatus","object":"optional","assertionKind":"classification","scope":"Nexus","confidence":0.86,"assertedBy":"Steward","assertedAt":"2026-06-24T23:58:00Z","status":"provisional","rationale":"LOSM The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this usefulA raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.4.1identityStatusAlready covered, but worth repeating:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknown4.2responsibilityProfileWhat is this thingfor?JSON{"responsibilityProfile": {"primaryFunction":"semantic_memory","secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage","identity_canonicalization"],"boundedContext":"architecture_memory","antiResponsibilities": ["operational task queue","ephemeral working set"]}}SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:semantic_memoryoperational_memoryruntime_orchestrationservice_brokeringsemantic_executiondocument_ingestionprojection_generationgovernancecapability_augmentationui_interactionintegration_surfaceThis lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”4.3topologyRoleThis is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.SourcesYou said:Turn this into a concrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draft for Nexus.TopologyChatGPT said:Good — this is exactly the point where it should stop being conceptual and become something a compiler or generator can actually eat.Below is aconcrete hybrid TypeSpec + JSON-LD framingforNexus.Topology. I’m going to structure it so:TypeSpec = authoritative schema contract (generation-friendly)JSON-LD context = semantic graph binding (self-describing + queryable)Design stays aligned with your CIR/CER/CGEL/WRP world1. TopologyEdge{id:string;from:string;to:string;relationshipType:RelationshipType;strength?:string;cardinality?:string;semanticMeaning?:string;activationConditions?:string[];evidenceRefs?:string[];}// ----------------------------// ASSERTION LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyAssertion{id:string;subject:string;predicate:string;object:string;assertionKind:AssertionKind;scope:string;confidence:float32;status:string;// provisional | accepted | rejectedrationale?:string;supportedBy?:string[];assertedBy:string;assertedAt:utcDateTime;}// ----------------------------// EVIDENCE LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyEvidence{id:string;evidenceKind:EvidenceKind;capturedAt:utcDateTime;sourceRef:string;summary:string;supports:string[];confidence:float32;}// ----------------------------// PROJECTION LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyProjection{id:string;name:string;projectionKind:string;focusEntities?:string[];focusAssertions?:string[];derivedFromSnapshot:string;}// ----------------------------// PROMOTION / GOVERNANCE// ----------------------------model TopologyPromotionDecision{id:string;candidateAssertion:string;decision:string;// accepted | rejected | deferreddecidedBy:string;decidedAt:utcDateTime;resultingAssertion?:string;notes?:string;}}3. JSON-LD sample instance (LOSM example)This shows how the schema actuallybehavesas a graph.JSON{"@context":"nexus-topology.context.jsonld","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityId":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","identityStatus":"optional","topologyRole":"semantic_execution_engine","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"description":"Optional semantic execution engine used for graph-based reasoning and process orchestration.","implementedBy": ["repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-host"],"runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","external":true}}4. What this buys you immediatelyThis schema is not just representation — it gives youthree hard capabilities:1. Self-inventoryNexus can answer:what existswhere it liveswhat kind of thing it is2. Self-interpretationBecause of:identityStatusTopologyAssertionTopologyRoleNexus can say:“LOSM is optional”“PEB is constitutive”“OpenCode is external harness state”3. Self-topology explanationBecause of edges + assertions:“what uses what”“why it exists”“what would break if removed”5. The key design win (this is the important part)This schema cleanly separates:Structure (truth)entitiesedgesMeaning (interpretation)assertionsidentityStatustopologyRoleEvidence (epistemology)why we believe anything at allThat separation is what makes:“Nexus can tell us what it is and how it thinks about itself”actually work instead of becoming a static diagram.If you want the next step, the natural continuation is:a query layer for this model(e.g. “show all optional capability modules and what they depend on” or “explain Nexus core identity boundary”), which is where it starts becoming a real introspection engine instead of just a schema.

Yes — that’s a cleaner cut.

That means LOSM stops being part of theidentity layerof Nexus/Assistance and becomes part of thecapability layer.

## The distinction I’d make

### Core system

What Assistanceiseven in its leanest valid form:

- the semantic contracts you want preserved across the bridgeCIRCERCGELWRP
the semantic contracts you want preserved across the bridge

- CIR
CIR

- CER
CER

- CGEL
CGEL

- WRP
WRP

- the base runtime / orchestration / governance model
the base runtime / orchestration / governance model

- the PGV successor to PEB
the PGV successor to PEB

- whatever minimal execution + memory + coordination substrate makes “Assistance” still be Assistance
whatever minimal execution + memory + coordination substrate makes “Assistance” still be Assistance

### Optional process modules / upgrades

What Assistance canuseif present, but does not require to exist as itself:

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

- probably other heavier cognition / perception / semantic-processing subsystems later
probably other heavier cognition / perception / semantic-processing subsystems later

So instead of saying “Assistance includes LOSM,” the better statement is:

> Assistance defines a contract for semantic execution augmentation, and LOSM is one implementation of that contract.

Assistance defines a contract for semantic execution augmentation, and LOSM is one implementation of that contract.

That’s a much better place for it.

---

# Why this is a useful move

Because it prevents LOSM from becoming a mandatory dependency of the whole system.

If LOSM stays “inside” Assistance, then every Assistance deployment implicitly inherits:

- its complexity
its complexity

- its process model
its process model

- its runtime assumptions
its runtime assumptions

- its maintenance burden
its maintenance burden

- its startup / resource cost
its startup / resource cost

If LOSM becomes an optional upgrade, then Assistance can exist in tiers:

## Assistance Core

- orchestration
orchestration

- role/governance/runtime
role/governance/runtime

- shared IR/contracts
shared IR/contracts

- durable memory/governance substrate
durable memory/governance substrate

- enough to do real work
enough to do real work

## Assistance + LOSM

- richer semantic execution / graph morphism / lifecycle intelligence / whatever subset of LOSM survives extraction
richer semantic execution / graph morphism / lifecycle intelligence / whatever subset of LOSM survives extraction

## Assistance + Vision

- multimodal / visual / perception-specific capabilities
multimodal / visual / perception-specific capabilities

## Assistance + LOSM + Vision

- the full loaded build
the full loaded build

That’s a much healthier product/system topology than “everything is fused into the definition of the platform.”

---

# I’d name the split explicitly

## Identity-defining components

Without these, it’s not Assistance:

- shared semantic contracts
shared semantic contracts

- base runtime/orchestration/governance substrate
base runtime/orchestration/governance substrate

- PGV / durable project-governance memory layer
PGV / durable project-governance memory layer

- whatever minimal execution spine is required
whatever minimal execution spine is required

## Capability modules

These enhance Assistance but do not define it:

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

- maybe future planner/analysis accelerators, specialized graph engines, etc.
maybe future planner/analysis accelerators, specialized graph engines, etc.

---

# That also clarifies Nexus’s relationship to LOSM

If Nexus canuseLOSM withoutbeingLOSM, then LOSM is really closer to:

- a semantic execution engine
a semantic execution engine

- a cognition/graph-processing subsystem
a cognition/graph-processing subsystem

- a service/module that can be mounted into multiple hosts
a service/module that can be mounted into multiple hosts

So Nexus and Assistance becomehosts / consumersof LOSM, not containers for it.

That’s a much stronger architectural position because it means LOSM can have its own lifecycle and contract.

---

# The bridge taxonomy gets sharper if you add one more category

Earlier we had:

- Preserve
Preserve

- Refine
Refine

- Extract
Extract

I’d now split “Extract” into two subtypes:

## Extract as foundational service

A thing pulled out because multiple systems should depend on it.

- LOSM
LOSM

## Extract as optional capability module

A thing pulled out because it’s valuable but not identity-defining.

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

LOSM may actually be both:

- extracted from Nexus internals
extracted from Nexus internals

- positioned as an optional capability module for Assistance
positioned as an optional capability module for Assistance

---

# So the Assistance picture starts to look like this

## Assistance Core

- CIR / CER / CGEL / WRP compatibility substrate
CIR / CER / CGEL / WRP compatibility substrate

- runtime / governance / orchestration
runtime / governance / orchestration

- PGV
PGV

- change log / continuity / execution spine
change log / continuity / execution spine

- minimal multirole agent system
minimal multirole agent system

## Capability Modules

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

- anything else that augments semantic perception/execution
anything else that augments semantic perception/execution

## Adapters / contracts

- the interfaces that let Core call into those modules without hard-wiring itself to them
the interfaces that let Core call into those modules without hard-wiring itself to them

---

# The sentence I’d use in your docs

Something like:

> LOSM and Vision are not constitutive parts of Assistance. They are optional capability modules that Assistance can mount through stable process contracts.

LOSM and Vision are not constitutive parts of Assistance. They are optional capability modules that Assistance can mount through stable process contracts.

And for Nexus:

> Nexus may host or consume LOSM, but LOSM is no longer treated as part of Nexus’s defining architecture.

Nexus may host or consume LOSM, but LOSM is no longer treated as part of Nexus’s defining architecture.

That gives you a clean way to keep LOSM valuable without letting it dominate the shape of either platform.

If you want, I canturn this into aCore vs Capability Module boundary docfor Assistance, witha table that classifies CIR/CER/CGEL/WRP/PGV/LOSM/Vision into:

- Core identity
Core identity

- shared compatibility substrate
shared compatibility substrate

- optional capability module
optional capability module

- external process/service
external process/service

hand-updating, but a system that can answer, from its own artifacts and state:what components existwhich ones are constitutive vs optionalwhat each component is used forwhat depends on whatwhat it believes its topology is right nowwhere its own uncertainty / drift / contradictions areThat’s a much more useful milestone than prematurely deciding the Assistance packaging.The real near-term objectiveYou want Nexus to be able to produce something like:“Current System Topology Report”with sections such as:1.Constitutive components“What Nexus currently believes itismade of”2.Consumed modules / optional capabilities“What Nexus uses but does not consider part of its core identity”3.Responsibility map“What each component is for”4.Dependency graph“Who calls whom / who depends on whom / what data crosses the boundary”5.Confidence / provenance“How Nexus knows this, and how sure it is”6.Known topology ambiguities“LOSM may be embedded vs externalized”, “OpenCode is harness-only”, etc.That’s the artifact that will actually let you cross the bridge cleanly later.I think this implies a pretty specific sequencePhase 1 — Make Nexus observable to itselfBefore deciding what Assistance keeps, get Nexus to enumerate:componentsroles of componentscontracts / interfacesdependenciesexecution paths“is/uses” distinctionsThis is basically:self-inventory + self-topology + self-interpretationPhase 2 — Let Nexus classify its own partsOnce it can enumerate, it can start labeling things like:coreadapterexternal tooloptional capabilitylegacy artifactexperimental subsystemThis is where LOSM starts getting identified as:“used by Nexus” rather than “part of Nexus”but you letNexus tell you that, rather than imposing it too early.Phase 3 — Use that self-description as Assistance inputThen the Assistance bridge becomes grounded in evidence:“Nexus says these are core”“Nexus says these are optional”“these interfaces are stable enough to preserve”“these modules are really external consumers/providers”That’s much better than deciding by intuition alone.What Nexus needs in order to tell you thisFor Nexus to answer “what do I contain / what do I use / what do I think my topology is?”, it needs at least three layers of representation:1.Inventory layerA registry of components/artifacts/modules/services/repos/processes.Questions it should answer:what exists?where does it live?what type of thing is it?2.Relationship layerLinks like:containsdepends_oncallsimplementsgeneratespersists_toreads_fromoptional_forconsumes_serviceprovides_capabilityQuestions it should answer:what uses LOSM?what depends on PGV?is Vision mounted or constitutive?3.Interpretation layerA higher-level “what is this for?” view.Questions it should answer:is this identity-defining or auxiliary?is this runtime, storage, governance, cognition, UI, or integration?does Nexus believe this component is core, optional, experimental, or legacy?That’s the piece that turns a graph into a self-model.In other words: Nexus needs a topology self-model, not just a code graphA code graph can tell you:importspackagesfilesmaybe service boundariesBut it won’t reliably tell you:“LOSM is optional”“PEB is being superseded conceptually by PGV”“OpenCode is a harness concern, not a system role model”“this MCP is part of the integration surface, not the core ontology”That requiresinterpretive metadata, not just structural extraction.So I’d phrase the milestone like thisMilestone: Nexus Topology Self-AwarenessNexus can generate a current, reviewable self-description of:what it containswhat it useswhat each thing is forhow the pieces connectwhich pieces it considers core vs optional vs externalhow confident it is in those assertionsOnce that exists, Assistance planning gets much less speculative.And the nice part is: this aligns directly with Nebula / KG / harvested materialBecause the “Nexus tells us what it is” problem sounds like it can be assembled from exactly the stuff you’ve been building toward:harvested docs and transcriptsrepo scanning / code indexingproject records in NebulaKG / topology relationshipschange logs / evolution historyrole-specific interpretations layered over the same substrateSo rather than writing a static architecture doc, you’re really aiming for aqueryable architectural self-model.That’s the right target.If you want, next step I cansketch theTopology Self-Model schemafor Nexus— not the final Assistance thing, but the intermediate model Nexus would need in Nebula/KG to answer:“what am I?”“what do I use?”“what is core vs optional?”“what does component X do for me?”Sources

Yes — and that’s the better immediate target than trying to freeze the Assistance boundary too early.

What you want first isNexus self-description.

Not a documentaboutNexus that you keep hand-updating, but a system that can answer, from its own artifacts and state:

- what components exist
what components exist

- which ones are constitutive vs optional
which ones are constitutive vs optional

- what each component is used for
what each component is used for

- what depends on what
what depends on what

- what it believes its topology is right now
what it believes its topology is right now

- where its own uncertainty / drift / contradictions are
where its own uncertainty / drift / contradictions are

That’s a much more useful milestone than prematurely deciding the Assistance packaging.

---

# The real near-term objective

You want Nexus to be able to produce something like:

## “Current System Topology Report”

with sections such as:

### 1.Constitutive components

“What Nexus currently believes itismade of”

### 2.Consumed modules / optional capabilities

“What Nexus uses but does not consider part of its core identity”

### 3.Responsibility map

“What each component is for”

### 4.Dependency graph

“Who calls whom / who depends on whom / what data crosses the boundary”

### 5.Confidence / provenance

“How Nexus knows this, and how sure it is”

### 6.Known topology ambiguities

“LOSM may be embedded vs externalized”, “OpenCode is harness-only”, etc.

That’s the artifact that will actually let you cross the bridge cleanly later.

---

# I think this implies a pretty specific sequence

## Phase 1 — Make Nexus observable to itself

Before deciding what Assistance keeps, get Nexus to enumerate:

- components
components

- roles of components
roles of components

- contracts / interfaces
contracts / interfaces

- dependencies
dependencies

- execution paths
execution paths

- “is/uses” distinctions
“is/uses” distinctions

This is basically:

> self-inventory + self-topology + self-interpretation

self-inventory + self-topology + self-interpretation

---

## Phase 2 — Let Nexus classify its own parts

Once it can enumerate, it can start labeling things like:

- core
core

- adapter
adapter

- external tool
external tool

- optional capability
optional capability

- legacy artifact
legacy artifact

- experimental subsystem
experimental subsystem

This is where LOSM starts getting identified as:

> “used by Nexus” rather than “part of Nexus”

“used by Nexus” rather than “part of Nexus”

but you letNexus tell you that, rather than imposing it too early.

---

## Phase 3 — Use that self-description as Assistance input

Then the Assistance bridge becomes grounded in evidence:

- “Nexus says these are core”
“Nexus says these are core”

- “Nexus says these are optional”
“Nexus says these are optional”

- “these interfaces are stable enough to preserve”
“these interfaces are stable enough to preserve”

- “these modules are really external consumers/providers”
“these modules are really external consumers/providers”

That’s much better than deciding by intuition alone.

---

# What Nexus needs in order to tell you this

For Nexus to answer “what do I contain / what do I use / what do I think my topology is?”, it needs at least three layers of representation:

## 1.Inventory layer

A registry of components/artifacts/modules/services/repos/processes.

Questions it should answer:

- what exists?
what exists?

- where does it live?
where does it live?

- what type of thing is it?
what type of thing is it?

---

## 2.Relationship layer

Links like:

- contains
contains

- depends_on
depends_on

- calls
calls

- implements
implements

- generates
generates

- persists_to
persists_to

- reads_from
reads_from

- optional_for
optional_for

- consumes_service
consumes_service

- provides_capability
provides_capability

Questions it should answer:

- what uses LOSM?
what uses LOSM?

- what depends on PGV?
what depends on PGV?

- is Vision mounted or constitutive?
is Vision mounted or constitutive?

---

## 3.Interpretation layer

A higher-level “what is this for?” view.

Questions it should answer:

- is this identity-defining or auxiliary?
is this identity-defining or auxiliary?

- is this runtime, storage, governance, cognition, UI, or integration?
is this runtime, storage, governance, cognition, UI, or integration?

- does Nexus believe this component is core, optional, experimental, or legacy?
does Nexus believe this component is core, optional, experimental, or legacy?

That’s the piece that turns a graph into a self-model.

---

# In other words: Nexus needs a topology self-model, not just a code graph

A code graph can tell you:

- imports
imports

- packages
packages

- files
files

- maybe service boundaries
maybe service boundaries

But it won’t reliably tell you:

- “LOSM is optional”
“LOSM is optional”

- “PEB is being superseded conceptually by PGV”
“PEB is being superseded conceptually by PGV”

- “OpenCode is a harness concern, not a system role model”
“OpenCode is a harness concern, not a system role model”

- “this MCP is part of the integration surface, not the core ontology”
“this MCP is part of the integration surface, not the core ontology”

That requiresinterpretive metadata, not just structural extraction.

---

# So I’d phrase the milestone like this

## Milestone: Nexus Topology Self-Awareness

Nexus can generate a current, reviewable self-description of:

- what it contains
what it contains

- what it uses
what it uses

- what each thing is for
what each thing is for

- how the pieces connect
how the pieces connect

- which pieces it considers core vs optional vs external
which pieces it considers core vs optional vs external

- how confident it is in those assertions
how confident it is in those assertions

Once that exists, Assistance planning gets much less speculative.

---

Because the “Nexus tells us what it is” problem sounds like it can be assembled from exactly the stuff you’ve been building toward:

- harvested docs and transcripts
harvested docs and transcripts

- repo scanning / code indexing
repo scanning / code indexing

- project records in Nebula
project records in Nebula

- KG / topology relationships
KG / topology relationships

- change logs / evolution history
change logs / evolution history

- role-specific interpretations layered over the same substrate
role-specific interpretations layered over the same substrate

So rather than writing a static architecture doc, you’re really aiming for aqueryable architectural self-model.

That’s the right target.

Core schema objects2.1TopologySnapshotThis is the anchor record for a reportable state of the world.JSON{"@type":"TopologySnapshot","snapshotId":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","system":"Nexus","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","scope": {"repos": ["nexus","conduit","tackle","losm-*"],"branches": ["main","feature/..."],"workRequests": ["WR-123","WR-124"],"harvestWindow": {"from":"2026-06-20T00:00:00Z","to":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}},"topologyStateVector": {"entityCount":87,"edgeCount":241,"assertionCount":64,"confidenceScore":0.78},"defaultProjection": {"inventoryView":"view://topology/inventory/core","dependencyView":"view://topology/dependencies/runtime","identityView":"view://topology/identity-vs-optional"},"containsEntities": ["entity:peb","entity:nebula","entity:losm","entity:wrp"],"containsEdges": ["edge:1","edge:2","edge:3"],"containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional","assertion:peb-canonical"],"derivedFrom": ["harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24","harvest:docs-2026-06-24","gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"]}Why this mattersThis gives you thesnapshot-able state vectoryou were talking about around levels / frozen graph state / pointer projection. The snapshot is the thing Nebula can review, diff, and surface; the semantic identity of the nodes/edges/assertions belongs on the PEB/KG side.2.2TopologyEntityA general node type with subtype classification.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEntity","@id":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","subtype":"semantic_execution_engine","lifecycleState":"active","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"identityStatus":"optional","runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","hostedBy":null,"consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]},"sourceOfTruth": {"semantic":"PEB","operational":"Nebula"},"descriptions": {"summary":"Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."},"tags": ["bridge-candidate","optional-upgrade","extractable"],"ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],"implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-host"]}Key fieldsentityKindOne of:systemrepomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolmcpschemairmemory_substratecapability_moduleharness_featuredocument_corpusexternal_systemui_surfacedatabaseprojectionsnapshotidentityStatusThis is critical. It should answer “is this part of what Nexusis?”Suggested enum:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknownExamples:PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrateLOSM / Vision→optionalOpenCode mode state→harness_feature+external_dependencyGitHub / Ollama / OpenCode itself→external_dependency2.3TopologyEdgeA typed relationship with optional runtime / contract metadata.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEdge","@id":"edge:nexus-uses-losm","from":"entity:nexus-runtime","to":"entity:losm","relationshipType":"uses","cardinality":"0..1","strength":"optional","directionality":"outbound","semanticMeaning":"Nexus can invoke LOSM as a semantic execution capability.","activationConditions": ["workload requires semantic execution / graph processing"],"evidenceRefs": ["evidence:losm-integration-doc","evidence:repo-scan-losm-client"]}Relationship types I’d define up frontStructuralcontainspart_ofhostsimplemented_byDependency / executiondepends_onusesinvokesreads_fromwrites_topersists_toqueriesroutes_throughbootstrapsSemantic / representationalprojects_toderived_fromtranslated_tomirrorssupersedesbridges_toGovernance / identitygoverned_bycanonicalized_byclassified_asoptional_forrequired_forTopology-specificexposed_viamounted_intoconsumed_byshares_contract_with2.4TopologyAssertionThis is where the self-model stops being just a code graph and starts becomingwhat Nexus thinks about itself.JSON{"@type":"TopologyAssertion","@id":"assertion:losm-optional","subject":"entity:losm","predicate":"identityStatus","object":"optional","assertionKind":"classification","scope":"Nexus","confidence":0.86,"assertedBy":"Steward","assertedAt":"2026-06-24T23:58:00Z","status":"provisional","rationale":"LOSM is increasingly viewed as a capability Nexus can use rather than part of Nexus’s defining architecture.","supportedBy": ["evidence:conversation-2026-06-24-losm-optional","evidence:dependency-graph-losm-boundary"]}Assertion kindsclassificationresponsibilityidentity_boundarydependency_interpretationbridge_decisiontopology_claimdeprecation_claimuncertainty_claimExamples:“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“Nebula is operational/work memory”“LOSM is optional capability module”“PGV supersedes PEB in Assistance but must preserve semantic continuity”“OpenCode role state is not a project role”2.5TopologyEvidenceThis is how you keep the model from becoming hand-wavy.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEvidence","@id":"evidence:repo-scan-losm-client","evidenceKind":"repo_scan","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T22:10:00Z","source": {"repo":"nexus","path":"services/semantic/losm_client.py","commit":"abc123"},"summary":"Nexus runtime imports LOSM client and conditionally dispatches semantic execution requests to it.","supports": ["assertion:losm-optional","edge:nexus-uses-losm"],"confidence":0.91}Evidence kindsrepo_scanimport_graphruntime_observationconfig_observationdocument_passagetranscript_quotechange_log_eventsteward_annotationmanual_curator_note3. The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this usefulA raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.4.1identityStatusAlready covered, but worth repeating:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknown4.2responsibilityProfileWhat is this thingfor?JSON{"responsibilityProfile": {"primaryFunction":"semantic_memory","secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage","identity_canonicalization"],"boundedContext":"architecture_memory","antiResponsibilities": ["operational task queue","ephemeral working set"]}}SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:semantic_memoryoperational_memoryruntime_orchestrationservice_brokeringsemantic_executiondocument_ingestionprojection_generationgovernancecapability_augmentationui_interactionintegration_surfaceThis lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”4.3topologyRoleThis is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.Sources

Thought for 6sAbsolutely. I’d sketch it as aJSON-LD / graph-native topology self-modelthat lives on thePEB/KG side as meaning + identity + provenance, withNebula as the operational/work projection. In other words:Nexus should describe itself semantically in the PEB, and Nebula should carry the reviewable operational slices, scans, and snapshots that feed or project from it.What follows isnot“final Assistance ontology.” It’s theintermediate Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM): the minimum model Nexus needs in order to answer:What do I contain?What do I use?What is each thing for?What is core vs optional vs external?What do I believe my topology is right now?How do I know that, and how confident am I?Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM) — Schema Sketch v0.10. Design goalsThe TSM should let Nexus representboth structure and interpretation.It needs to capture:Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems existRelationships— how they depend on or use each otherInterpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curationTemporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what roleThat means the model needsthree layers:Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placementsInterpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classificationEvidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context1.
