The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.Sources

Thought for 6sAbsolutely. I’d sketch it as aJSON-LD / graph-native topology self-modelthat lives on thePEB/KG side as meaning + identity + provenance, withNebula as the operational/work projection. In other words:Nexus should describe itself semantically in the PEB, and Nebula should carry the reviewable operational slices, scans, and snapshots that feed or project from it.What follows isnot“final Assistance ontology.” It’s theintermediate Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM): the minimum model Nexus needs in order to answer:What do I contain?What do I use?What is each thing for?What is core vs optional vs external?What do I believe my topology is right now?How do I know that, and how confident am I?Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM) — Schema Sketch v0.10. Design goalsThe TSM should let Nexus representboth structure and interpretation.It needs to capture:Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems existRelationships— how they depend on or use each otherInterpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curationTemporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what roleThat means the model needsthree layers:Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placementsInterpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classificationEvidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context1. Top-level object modelI’d center it on aTopologySnapshotthat contains a graph ofTopologyEntities,TopologyEdges, andTopologyAssertions.Primary classesA.TopologySnapshotA frozen view of “what Nexus believes its topology is” at a moment in time.B.TopologyEntityA node in the topology graph:repomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolMCPschema/IRdocument corpusexternal systemcapability moduleharness concernC.TopologyEdgeA typed relationship between two entities:containsdepends_onusesinvokeshostspersists_toreads_fromwrites_toprojects_toderives_fromsupersedesbridges_tooptional_forrequired_forgoverned_byexposed_viaD.TopologyAssertionAn interpretable claim about an entity or relationship:“LOSM is optional for Nexus”“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“OpenCode role state is a harness concern, not a project role”“CGEL is a portable semantic contract likely to survive the bridge”E.TopologyEvidenceA provenance-bearing observation supporting an assertion:repo scan resultimport graphdocs passagechange log eventsteward classificationtranscript quotemanual declaration2. Core schema objects2.1TopologySnapshotThis is the anchor record for a reportable state of the world.JSON{"@type":"TopologySnapshot","snapshotId":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","system":"Nexus","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","scope": {"repos": ["nexus","conduit","tackle","losm-*"],"branches": ["main","feature/..."],"workRequests": ["WR-123","WR-124"],"harvestWindow": {"from":"2026-06-20T00:00:00Z","to":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}},"topologyStateVector": {"entityCount":87,"edgeCount":241,"assertionCount":64,"confidenceScore":0.78},"defaultProjection": {"inventoryView":"view://topology/inventory/core","dependencyView":"view://topology/dependencies/runtime","identityView":"view://topology/identity-vs-optional"},"containsEntities": ["entity:peb","entity:nebula","entity:losm","entity:wrp"],"containsEdges": ["edge:1","edge:2","edge:3"],"containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional","assertion:peb-canonical"],"derivedFrom": ["harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24","harvest:docs-2026-06-24","gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"]}Why this mattersThis gives you thesnapshot-able state vectoryou were talking about around levels / frozen graph state / pointer projection. The snapshot is the thing Nebula can review, diff, and surface; the semantic identity of the nodes/edges/assertions belongs on the PEB/KG side.2.2TopologyEntityA general node type with subtype classification.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEntity","@id":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","subtype":"semantic_execution_engine","lifecycleState":"active","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"identityStatus":"optional","runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","hostedBy":null,"consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]},"sourceOfTruth": {"semantic":"PEB","operational":"Nebula"},"descriptions": {"summary":"Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."},"tags": ["bridge-candidate","optional-upgrade","extractable"],"ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],"implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-host"]}Key fieldsentityKindOne of:systemrepomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolmcpschemairmemory_substratecapability_moduleharness_featuredocument_corpusexternal_systemui_surfacedatabaseprojectionsnapshotidentityStatusThis is critical. It should answer “is this part of what Nexusis?”Suggested enum:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknownExamples:PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrateLOSM / Vision→optionalOpenCode mode state→harness_feature+external_dependencyGitHub / Ollama / OpenCode itself→external_dependency2.3TopologyEdgeA typed relationship with optional runtime / contract metadata.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEdge","@id":"edge:nexus-uses-losm","from":"entity:nexus-runtime","to":"entity:losm","relationshipType":"uses","cardinality":"0..1","strength":"optional","directionality":"outbound","semanticMeaning":"Nexus can invoke LOSM as a semantic execution capability.","activationConditions": ["workload requires semantic execution / graph processing"],"evidenceRefs": ["evidence:losm-integration-doc","evidence:repo-scan-losm-client"]}Relationship types I’d define up frontStructuralcontainspart_ofhostsimplemented_byDependency / executiondepends_onusesinvokesreads_fromwrites_topersists_toqueriesroutes_throughbootstrapsSemantic / representationalprojects_toderived_fromtranslated_tomirrorssupersedesbridges_toGovernance / identitygoverned_bycanonicalized_byclassified_asoptional_forrequired_forTopology-specificexposed_viamounted_intoconsumed_byshares_contract_with2.4TopologyAssertionThis is where the self-model stops being just a code graph and starts becomingwhat Nexus thinks about itself.JSON{"@type":"TopologyAssertion","@id":"assertion:losm-optional","subject":"entity:losm","predicate":"identityStatus","object":"optional","assertionKind":"classification","scope":"Nexus","confidence":0.86,"assertedBy":"Steward","assertedAt":"2026-06-24T23:58:00Z","status":"provisional","rationale":"LOSM is increasingly viewed as a capability Nexus can use rather than part of Nexus’s defining architecture.","supportedBy": ["evidence:conversation-2026-06-24-losm-optional","evidence:dependency-graph-losm-boundary"]}Assertion kindsclassificationresponsibilityidentity_boundarydependency_interpretationbridge_decisiontopology_claimdeprecation_claimuncertainty_claimExamples:“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“Nebula is operational/work memory”“LOSM is optional capability module”“PGV supersedes PEB in Assistance but must preserve semantic continuity”“OpenCode role state is not a project role”2.5TopologyEvidenceThis is how you keep the model from becoming hand-wavy.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEvidence","@id":"evidence:repo-scan-losm-client","evidenceKind":"repo_scan","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T22:10:00Z","source": {"repo":"nexus","path":"services/semantic/losm_client.py","commit":"abc123"},"summary":"Nexus runtime imports LOSM client and conditionally dispatches semantic execution requests to it.","supports": ["assertion:losm-optional","edge:nexus-uses-losm"],"confidence":0.91}Evidence kindsrepo_scanimport_graphruntime_observationconfig_observationdocument_passagetranscript_quotechange_log_eventsteward_annotationmanual_curator_note3. The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this usefulA raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.4.1identityStatusAlready covered, but worth repeating:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknown4.2responsibilityProfileWhat is this thingfor?JSON{"responsibilityProfile": {"primaryFunction":"semantic_memory","secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage","identity_canonicalization"],"boundedContext":"architecture_memory","antiResponsibilities": ["operational task queue","ephemeral working set"]}}SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:semantic_memoryoperational_memoryruntime_orchestrationservice_brokeringsemantic_executiondocument_ingestionprojection_generationgovernancecapability_augmentationui_interactionintegration_surfaceThis lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”4.3topologyRoleThis is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.

Absolutely. I’d sketch it as aJSON-LD / graph-native topology self-modelthat lives on thePEB/KG side as meaning + identity + provenance, withNebula as the operational/work projection. In other words:Nexus should describe itself semantically in the PEB, and Nebula should carry the reviewable operational slices, scans, and snapshots that feed or project from it.What follows isnot“final Assistance ontology.” It’s theintermediate Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM): the minimum model Nexus needs in order to answer:What do I contain?What do I use?What is each thing for?What is core vs optional vs external?What do I believe my topology is right now?How do I know that, and how confident am I?Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM) — Schema Sketch v0.10. Design goalsThe TSM should let Nexus representboth structure and interpretation.It needs to capture:Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems existRelationships— how they depend on or use each otherInterpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curationTemporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what roleThat means the model needsthree layers:Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placementsInterpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classificationEvidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context1. Top-level object modelI’d center it on aTopologySnapshotthat contains a graph ofTopologyEntities,TopologyEdges, andTopologyAssertions.Primary classesA.TopologySnapshotA frozen view of “what Nexus believes its topology is” at a moment in time.B.TopologyEntityA node in the topology graph:repomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolMCPschema/IRdocument corpusexternal systemcapability moduleharness concernC.TopologyEdgeA typed relationship between two entities:containsdepends_onusesinvokeshostspersists_toreads_fromwrites_toprojects_toderives_fromsupersedesbridges_tooptional_forrequired_forgoverned_byexposed_viaD.TopologyAssertionAn interpretable claim about an entity or relationship:“LOSM is optional for Nexus”“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“OpenCode role state is a harness concern, not a project role”“CGEL is a portable semantic contract likely to survive the bridge”E.TopologyEvidenceA provenance-bearing observation supporting an assertion:repo scan resultimport graphdocs passagechange log eventsteward classificationtranscript quotemanual declaration2. Core schema objects2.1TopologySnapshotThis is the anchor record for a reportable state of the world.JSON{"@type":"TopologySnapshot","snapshotId":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","system":"Nexus","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","scope": {"repos": ["nexus","conduit","tackle","losm-*"],"branches": ["main","feature/..."],"workRequests": ["WR-123","WR-124"],"harvestWindow": {"from":"2026-06-20T00:00:00Z","to":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}},"topologyStateVector": {"entityCount":87,"edgeCount":241,"assertionCount":64,"confidenceScore":0.78},"defaultProjection": {"inventoryView":"view://topology/inventory/core","dependencyView":"view://topology/dependencies/runtime","identityView":"view://topology/identity-vs-optional"},"containsEntities": ["entity:peb","entity:nebula","entity:losm","entity:wrp"],"containsEdges": ["edge:1","edge:2","edge:3"],"containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional","assertion:peb-canonical"],"derivedFrom": ["harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24","harvest:docs-2026-06-24","gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"]}Why this mattersThis gives you thesnapshot-able state vectoryou were talking about around levels / frozen graph state / pointer projection. The snapshot is the thing Nebula can review, diff, and surface; the semantic identity of the nodes/edges/assertions belongs on the PEB/KG side.2.2TopologyEntityA general node type with subtype classification.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEntity","@id":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","subtype":"semantic_execution_engine","lifecycleState":"active","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"identityStatus":"optional","runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","hostedBy":null,"consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]},"sourceOfTruth": {"semantic":"PEB","operational":"Nebula"},"descriptions": {"summary":"Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."},"tags": ["bridge-candidate","optional-upgrade","extractable"],"ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],"implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-host"]}Key fieldsentityKindOne of:systemrepomodulepackageserviceprocessruntimeagenttoolmcpschemairmemory_substratecapability_moduleharness_featuredocument_corpusexternal_systemui_surfacedatabaseprojectionsnapshotidentityStatusThis is critical. It should answer “is this part of what Nexusis?”Suggested enum:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknownExamples:PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrateLOSM / Vision→optionalOpenCode mode state→harness_feature+external_dependencyGitHub / Ollama / OpenCode itself→external_dependency2.3TopologyEdgeA typed relationship with optional runtime / contract metadata.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEdge","@id":"edge:nexus-uses-losm","from":"entity:nexus-runtime","to":"entity:losm","relationshipType":"uses","cardinality":"0..1","strength":"optional","directionality":"outbound","semanticMeaning":"Nexus can invoke LOSM as a semantic execution capability.","activationConditions": ["workload requires semantic execution / graph processing"],"evidenceRefs": ["evidence:losm-integration-doc","evidence:repo-scan-losm-client"]}Relationship types I’d define up frontStructuralcontainspart_ofhostsimplemented_byDependency / executiondepends_onusesinvokesreads_fromwrites_topersists_toqueriesroutes_throughbootstrapsSemantic / representationalprojects_toderived_fromtranslated_tomirrorssupersedesbridges_toGovernance / identitygoverned_bycanonicalized_byclassified_asoptional_forrequired_forTopology-specificexposed_viamounted_intoconsumed_byshares_contract_with2.4TopologyAssertionThis is where the self-model stops being just a code graph and starts becomingwhat Nexus thinks about itself.JSON{"@type":"TopologyAssertion","@id":"assertion:losm-optional","subject":"entity:losm","predicate":"identityStatus","object":"optional","assertionKind":"classification","scope":"Nexus","confidence":0.86,"assertedBy":"Steward","assertedAt":"2026-06-24T23:58:00Z","status":"provisional","rationale":"LOSM is increasingly viewed as a capability Nexus can use rather than part of Nexus’s defining architecture.","supportedBy": ["evidence:conversation-2026-06-24-losm-optional","evidence:dependency-graph-losm-boundary"]}Assertion kindsclassificationresponsibilityidentity_boundarydependency_interpretationbridge_decisiontopology_claimdeprecation_claimuncertainty_claimExamples:“PEB is canonical semantic memory”“Nebula is operational/work memory”“LOSM is optional capability module”“PGV supersedes PEB in Assistance but must preserve semantic continuity”“OpenCode role state is not a project role”2.5TopologyEvidenceThis is how you keep the model from becoming hand-wavy.JSON{"@type":"TopologyEvidence","@id":"evidence:repo-scan-losm-client","evidenceKind":"repo_scan","capturedAt":"2026-06-24T22:10:00Z","source": {"repo":"nexus","path":"services/semantic/losm_client.py","commit":"abc123"},"summary":"Nexus runtime imports LOSM client and conditionally dispatches semantic execution requests to it.","supports": The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. kindsrepo_scanimport_graphruntime_observationconfig_observationdocument_passagetranscript_quotechange_log_eventsteward_annotationmanual_curator_note3. The specific concepts Nexus needs to representHere’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.3.1 Systems and memory substratesSystemNexusAssistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)LOSM (if treated as standalone system)Vision (if standalone)MemorySubstratePEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenanceNebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slicesKG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of itExecution ContextThought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)These need explicit relationships like:PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertionsNebula stores TopologySnapshotsTopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs3.2 Contract / IR artifactsThese are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.CIRCERCGELWRPWorkRequest IREpistemicQuery IRExecutionReceiptmaybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevantThese should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.Example:JSON{"@id":"entity:wrp","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityKind":"ir","name":"WRP","identityStatus":"shared_substrate","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"]}And then edges like:Nexus uses WRPAssistance bridges_to WRPConduit emits CERCIR derives_from WorkRequest3.3 Runtime / service / process componentsThese are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.Examples:Nexus runtime / broker / gatewayTackleConduitNebula servicesLOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponentsMCP integrationscrawlers/importers/harvestersUI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)These should have:runtime placementrepo ownershipinterfaces exposed / consumedpersistence relationshipscapability claims3.4 Harness / external tooling concernsThese matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.Examples:OpenCodeClaude Code / other harnessesOllamaPortainerGitDockerThese should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.This is where you capture things like:OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project roleBP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identityThat matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this usefulA raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.4.1identityStatusAlready covered, but worth repeating:constitutiveshared_substrateoptionalexternal_dependencyexperimentallegacyunknown4.2responsibilityProfileWhat is this thingfor?JSON{"responsibilityProfile": {"primaryFunction":"semantic_memory","secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage","identity_canonicalization"],"boundedContext":"architecture_memory","antiResponsibilities": ["operational task queue","ephemeral working set"]}}SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:semantic_memoryoperational_memoryruntime_orchestrationservice_brokeringsemantic_executiondocument_ingestionprojection_generationgovernancecapability_augmentationui_interactionintegration_surfaceThis lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”4.3topologyRoleThis is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.Suggested values:core_runtimememory_substratecapability_moduleadapterprojection_layergovernance_layerexternal_toolingingestion_pipelineintegration_boundaryThat gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projectionsYou’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection indexMeaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”Examples:core_vs_optional_viewruntime_dependency_viewmemory_flow_viewbridge_to_assistance_viewharness_vs_system_boundary_viewmcp_usage_view5.2 AddTopologyProjectionA reusable projection/view definition over the graph.JSON{"@type":"TopologyProjection","@id":"projection:core-vs-optional","name":"Core vs Optional Components","projectionKind":"classification_view","focus": {"entityFilter": ["system","capability_module","memory_substrate","ir"],"assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]},"derivedFromSnapshot":"topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.6.1 Core identity / substrate candidatesNexusNebulaPEBKG(if distinct)WRPCIRCERCGELConduitTackle6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidatesLOSMVision6.3 External / harness / environment entitiesOpenCodeOllamaGitDockerPortainer6.4 Projection / ingestion entitiesDocLingDocLanghtml_importerGraphBuilderharvested transcript corpus / docs corpusThat’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from thisOnce the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:“Describe your current topology”And produce something like:A. Core / constitutive componentsNexus runtimeNebulaPEBWRP / CIR / CER / CGELConduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)B. Optional capability modulesLOSMVisionC. External dependencies / harness concernsOpenCodeOllamaGit / DockerD. Memory and data flowharvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projectionsE. Known ambiguities“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”F. Confidence“Core identity classifications: high confidence”“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”That’s the actual practical output of the model.8. What I wouldnotdo yetI would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:Speed 1 — curated semantic topologyA relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:systemsmemory substratesIRs/contractsmajor runtime componentscapability modulesexternal/harness dependenciesSpeed 2 — harvested structural observationsRepo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.That matches your existing distinction between:what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEBwhat we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecisionBecause a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”JSON{"@type":"TopologyPromotionDecision","@id":"promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24","candidateAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional-candidate","decision":"accepted","decidedBy":"Steward","decidedAt":"2026-06-24T23:59:00Z","resultingAssertion":"assertion:losm-optional","notes":"LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}That gives you a clean governance seam:harvests proposeSteward/Nexus promotesPEB holds the canonical assertion10. My recommended v0 schema packageIf I were naming the initial schema bundle, I’d probably make aNexus.Topologypackage with these primary types:Nexus.Topologyv0TopologySnapshotTopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologyEvidenceTopologyProjectionTopologyPromotionDecisionSupporting enums / value objectsEntityKindIdentityStatusRelationshipTypeAssertionKindEvidenceKindResponsibilityProfileRuntimePlacementConfidenceScoreTopologyScope11. My recommended first-pass workflowIf you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:Step 1 — Curated seed inventoryManually seed 20–30 high-value entities:Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabularyStart with:containsusesdepends_onpersists_toprojects_tooptional_forrequired_forsupersedesStep 3 — Add identity assertionsFor each seeded entity, add:identityStatusresponsibilityProfiletopologyRoleStep 4 — Wire in evidence harvestAllow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:TopologyEvidencecandidateTopologyAssertionscandidateTopologyEdgesStep 5 — Add snapshot generationFreeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”If you want, I can do the next layer andturn this into aconcrete TypeSpec/JSON-LD draftforNexus.Topology— not just the conceptual schema, butactual type definitions for:TopologyEntityTopologyEdgeTopologyAssertionTopologySnapshotthe initial enums and value objects.

Absolutely. I’d sketch it as aJSON-LD / graph-native topology self-modelthat lives on thePEB/KG side as meaning + identity + provenance, withNebula as the operational/work projection. In other words:Nexus should describe itself semantically in the PEB, and Nebula should carry the reviewable operational slices, scans, and snapshots that feed or project from it.

What follows isnot“final Assistance ontology.” It’s theintermediate Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM): the minimum model Nexus needs in order to answer:

- What do I contain?
What do I contain?

- What do I use?
What do I use?

- What is each thing for?
What is each thing for?

- What is core vs optional vs external?
What is core vs optional vs external?

- What do I believe my topology is right now?
What do I believe my topology is right now?

- How do I know that, and how confident am I?
How do I know that, and how confident am I?

---

# Nexus Topology Self-Model (TSM) — Schema Sketch v0.1

## 0. Design goals

The TSM should let Nexus representboth structure and interpretation.

It needs to capture:

- Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems exist
Inventory— what components/artifacts/systems exist

- Relationships— how they depend on or use each other
Relationships— how they depend on or use each other

- Interpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.
Interpretation— what each thing isforand whether it isconstitutive,optional,external,experimental, etc.

- Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curation
Evidence / provenance— whether a claim came from code scan, docs, chat, steward assertion, or manual curation

- Temporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”
Temporal state— “what Nexus believes right now” as well as “what it believed at snapshot X”

- Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what role
Compatibility / bridge hints— which artifacts are likely to survive into Assistance and in what role

That means the model needsthree layers:

- Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placements
Topology Layer— nodes, interfaces, relationships, runtime placements

- Interpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classification
Interpretation Layer— role of component, purpose, identity/core/optional classification

- Evidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context
Evidence Layer— observations, assertions, confidence, source provenance, snapshot context

---

# 1. Top-level object model

I’d center it on aTopologySnapshotthat contains a graph ofTopologyEntities,TopologyEdges, andTopologyAssertions.

## Primary classes

### A.TopologySnapshot

A frozen view of “what Nexus believes its topology is” at a moment in time.

### B.TopologyEntity

A node in the topology graph:

- repo
repo

- module
module

- package
package

- service
service

- process
process

- runtime
runtime

- agent
agent

- tool
tool

- MCP
MCP

- schema/IR
schema/IR

- document corpus
document corpus

- external system
external system

- capability module
capability module

- harness concern
harness concern

### C.TopologyEdge

A typed relationship between two entities:

- contains
contains

- depends_on
depends_on

- uses
uses

- invokes
invokes

- hosts
hosts

- persists_to
persists_to

- reads_from
reads_from

- writes_to
writes_to

- projects_to
projects_to

- derives_from
derives_from

- supersedes
supersedes

- bridges_to
bridges_to

- optional_for
optional_for

- required_for
required_for

- governed_by
governed_by

- exposed_via
exposed_via

### D.TopologyAssertion

An interpretable claim about an entity or relationship:

- “LOSM is optional for Nexus”
“LOSM is optional for Nexus”

- “PEB is canonical semantic memory”
“PEB is canonical semantic memory”

- “OpenCode role state is a harness concern, not a project role”
“OpenCode role state is a harness concern, not a project role”

- “CGEL is a portable semantic contract likely to survive the bridge”
“CGEL is a portable semantic contract likely to survive the bridge”

### E.TopologyEvidence

A provenance-bearing observation supporting an assertion:

- repo scan result
repo scan result

- import graph
import graph

- docs passage
docs passage

- change log event
change log event

- steward classification
steward classification

- transcript quote
transcript quote

- manual declaration
manual declaration

# 2. Core schema objects

## 2.1TopologySnapshot

This is the anchor record for a reportable state of the world.

```
{  "@type": "TopologySnapshot",  "snapshotId": "topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "system": "Nexus",  "capturedAt": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "scope": {    "repos": ["nexus", "conduit", "tackle", "losm-*"],    "branches": ["main", "feature/..."],    "workRequests": ["WR-123", "WR-124"],    "harvestWindow": {      "from": "2026-06-20T00:00:00Z",      "to": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"    }  },  "topologyStateVector": {    "entityCount": 87,    "edgeCount": 241,    "assertionCount": 64,    "confidenceScore": 0.78  },  "defaultProjection": {    "inventoryView": "view://topology/inventory/core",    "dependencyView": "view://topology/dependencies/runtime",    "identityView": "view://topology/identity-vs-optional"  },  "containsEntities": ["entity:peb", "entity:nebula", "entity:losm", "entity:wrp"],  "containsEdges": ["edge:1", "edge:2", "edge:3"],  "containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional", "assertion:peb-canonical"],  "derivedFrom": [    "harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24",    "harvest:docs-2026-06-24",    "gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"  ]}
```

```
{  "@type": "TopologySnapshot",  "snapshotId": "topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "system": "Nexus",  "capturedAt": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "scope": {    "repos": ["nexus", "conduit", "tackle", "losm-*"],    "branches": ["main", "feature/..."],    "workRequests": ["WR-123", "WR-124"],    "harvestWindow": {      "from": "2026-06-20T00:00:00Z",      "to": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"    }  },  "topologyStateVector": {    "entityCount": 87,    "edgeCount": 241,    "assertionCount": 64,    "confidenceScore": 0.78  },  "defaultProjection": {    "inventoryView": "view://topology/inventory/core",    "dependencyView": "view://topology/dependencies/runtime",    "identityView": "view://topology/identity-vs-optional"  },  "containsEntities": ["entity:peb", "entity:nebula", "entity:losm", "entity:wrp"],  "containsEdges": ["edge:1", "edge:2", "edge:3"],  "containsAssertions": ["assertion:losm-optional", "assertion:peb-canonical"],  "derivedFrom": [    "harvest:repo-scan-2026-06-24",    "harvest:docs-2026-06-24",    "gcl:session-2026-06-24-tackle-conduit"  ]}
```

### Why this matters

This gives you thesnapshot-able state vectoryou were talking about around levels / frozen graph state / pointer projection. The snapshot is the thing Nebula can review, diff, and surface; the semantic identity of the nodes/edges/assertions belongs on the PEB/KG side.

## 2.2TopologyEntity

A general node type with subtype classification.

```
{  "@type": "TopologyEntity",  "@id": "entity:losm",  "name": "LOSM",  "entityKind": "capability_module",  "subtype": "semantic_execution_engine",  "lifecycleState": "active",  "systemAffinity": ["Nexus", "Assistance"],  "identityStatus": "optional",  "runtimePlacement": {    "placementKind": "external_process",    "hostedBy": null,    "consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]  },  "sourceOfTruth": {    "semantic": "PEB",    "operational": "Nebula"  },  "descriptions": {    "summary": "Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."  },  "tags": ["bridge-candidate", "optional-upgrade", "extractable"],  "ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],  "implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell", "repo:losm-kernel", "repo:losm-host"]}
```

```
{  "@type": "TopologyEntity",  "@id": "entity:losm",  "name": "LOSM",  "entityKind": "capability_module",  "subtype": "semantic_execution_engine",  "lifecycleState": "active",  "systemAffinity": ["Nexus", "Assistance"],  "identityStatus": "optional",  "runtimePlacement": {    "placementKind": "external_process",    "hostedBy": null,    "consumedVia": ["entity:nexus-runtime"]  },  "sourceOfTruth": {    "semantic": "PEB",    "operational": "Nebula"  },  "descriptions": {    "summary": "Semantic execution / graph-processing capability used by Nexus but not constitutive of Nexus identity."  },  "tags": ["bridge-candidate", "optional-upgrade", "extractable"],  "ownedContracts": ["contract:losm-api-v1"],  "implementedBy": ["repo:losm-shell", "repo:losm-kernel", "repo:losm-host"]}
```

One of:

This is critical. It should answer “is this part of what Nexusis?”

Suggested enum:

external_dependency

Examples:

- PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrate
PEB / Nebula / WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL→ probablyconstitutiveorshared_substrate

LOSM / Vision→optional

OpenCode mode state→harness_feature+external_dependency

## 2.4TopologyAssertion

# 3. The specific concepts Nexus needs to represent

Here’s the set ofentity categoriesI think the TSM absolutely needs to support foryourstack.

---

## 3.1 Systems and memory substrates

### System

- Nexus
Nexus

- Assistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)
Assistance (eventually, for bridge comparison)

- LOSM (if treated as standalone system)
LOSM (if treated as standalone system)

- Vision (if standalone)
Vision (if standalone)

### MemorySubstrate

- PEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenance
PEB— canonical semantic graph of meaning/identity/context/provenance

- Nebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slices
Nebula— operational/work memory, project records, reviewable slices

- KG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of it
KG— if distinct from PEB in practice, or a facet of it

- Execution Context
Execution Context

- Thought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)
Thought Context(if you want explicit representation of working memory surfaces)

These need explicit relationships like:

- PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertions
PEB canonicalizes TopologyAssertions

- Nebula stores TopologySnapshots
Nebula stores TopologySnapshots

- TopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs
TopologySnapshot projects_from PEB + repo scan + GCL + harvested docs

## 3.2 Contract / IR artifacts

These are likely first-class entities because they’re bridge-critical.

- CIR
CIR

- CER
CER

- CGEL
CGEL

- WRP
WRP

- WorkRequest IR
WorkRequest IR

- EpistemicQuery IR
EpistemicQuery IR

- ExecutionReceipt
ExecutionReceipt

- maybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevant
maybe DocLang / DocLing projections where relevant

These should be represented asentityKind = irorschema.

Example:

```
{  "@id": "entity:wrp",  "@type": "TopologyEntity",  "entityKind": "ir",  "name": "WRP",  "identityStatus": "shared_substrate",  "systemAffinity": ["Nexus", "Assistance"]}
```

```
{  "@id": "entity:wrp",  "@type": "TopologyEntity",  "entityKind": "ir",  "name": "WRP",  "identityStatus": "shared_substrate",  "systemAffinity": ["Nexus", "Assistance"]}
```

And then edges like:

- Nexus uses WRP
Nexus uses WRP

Assistance bridges_to WRP

- Conduit emits CER
Conduit emits CER

- CIR derives_from WorkRequest
CIR derives_from WorkRequest

## 3.3 Runtime / service / process components

These are the actual moving pieces of Nexus.

Examples:

- Nexus runtime / broker / gateway
Nexus runtime / broker / gateway

- Tackle
Tackle

- Conduit
Conduit

- Nebula services
Nebula services

- LOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponents
LOSM host / shell / kernel / store if still visible as subcomponents

- MCP integrations
MCP integrations

- crawlers/importers/harvesters
crawlers/importers/harvesters

- UI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)
UI surfaces (Throttler / Console if you want them in the topology)

These should have:

- runtime placement
runtime placement

- repo ownership
repo ownership

- interfaces exposed / consumed
interfaces exposed / consumed

- persistence relationships
persistence relationships

- capability claims
capability claims

---

## 3.4 Harness / external tooling concerns

These matter because you explicitly want Nexus to know what ispart of itselfvs what is just a tool around it.

Examples:

- OpenCode
OpenCode

- Claude Code / other harnesses
Claude Code / other harnesses

- Ollama
Ollama

- Portainer
Portainer

- Git
Git

- Docker
Docker

These should be represented asexternal dependencies or harness features, not core system nodes.

This is where you capture things like:

- OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"
OpenCode has harness feature "mode state"

- Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project role
Nexus does not treat OpenCode role state as project role

- BP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identity
BP can operate through OpenCode, but OpenCode mode is execution metadata, not topology identity

That matters because otherwise your topology self-model will confuse “thing Nexus uses” with “thing Nexus is.”

---

# 4. The interpretation layer: the fields that make this useful

A raw graph isn’t enough. EachTopologyEntityneeds a few interpretive fields.

## 4.1identityStatus

Already covered, but worth repeating:

- constitutive
constitutive

- shared_substrate
shared_substrate

- optional
optional

- external_dependency
external_dependency

- experimental
experimental

- legacy
legacy

- unknown
unknown

## 4.2responsibilityProfile

What is this thingfor?

```
{  "responsibilityProfile": {    "primaryFunction": "semantic_memory",    "secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage", "identity_canonicalization"],    "boundedContext": "architecture_memory",    "antiResponsibilities": [      "operational task queue",      "ephemeral working set"    ]  }}
```

```
{  "responsibilityProfile": {    "primaryFunction": "semantic_memory",    "secondaryFunctions": ["topology_assertion_storage", "identity_canonicalization"],    "boundedContext": "architecture_memory",    "antiResponsibilities": [      "operational task queue",      "ephemeral working set"    ]  }}
```

SuggestedprimaryFunctionvalues:

- semantic_memory
semantic_memory

- operational_memory
operational_memory

- runtime_orchestration
runtime_orchestration

- service_brokering
service_brokering

- semantic_execution
semantic_execution

- document_ingestion
document_ingestion

- projection_generation
projection_generation

- governance
governance

- capability_augmentation
capability_augmentation

- ui_interaction
ui_interaction

- integration_surface
integration_surface

This lets Nexus answer “what does this thing do for me?”

## 4.3topologyRole

This is different from agent role. This isthe role of a component in the topology.

Suggested values:

- core_runtime
core_runtime

- memory_substrate
memory_substrate

- capability_module
capability_module

- adapter
adapter

- projection_layer
projection_layer

- governance_layer
governance_layer

- external_tooling
external_tooling

- ingestion_pipeline
ingestion_pipeline

- integration_boundary
integration_boundary

That gives you a more stable classification than ad hoc tags.

---

# 5. Snapshots, levels, and default query projections

You’ve already got the idea ofsnapshots + pointer projections + stabilized coordinates / levelsfloating around. The TSM should lean into that.

## 5.1TopologySnapshotshould carry aprojection index

Meaning: “what are the canonical ways to look at this topology?”

Examples:

- core_vs_optional_view
core_vs_optional_view

- runtime_dependency_view
runtime_dependency_view

- memory_flow_view
memory_flow_view

- bridge_to_assistance_view
bridge_to_assistance_view

- harness_vs_system_boundary_view
harness_vs_system_boundary_view

- mcp_usage_view
mcp_usage_view

## 5.2 AddTopologyProjection

A reusable projection/view definition over the graph.

```
{  "@type": "TopologyProjection",  "@id": "projection:core-vs-optional",  "name": "Core vs Optional Components",  "projectionKind": "classification_view",  "focus": {    "entityFilter": ["system", "capability_module", "memory_substrate", "ir"],    "assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]  },  "derivedFromSnapshot": "topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}
```

```
{  "@type": "TopologyProjection",  "@id": "projection:core-vs-optional",  "name": "Core vs Optional Components",  "projectionKind": "classification_view",  "focus": {    "entityFilter": ["system", "capability_module", "memory_substrate", "ir"],    "assertionPredicates": ["identityStatus"]  },  "derivedFromSnapshot": "topo-2026-06-24T23:59:00Z"}
```

This gives you the reviewable surfaces in Nebula without making Nebula the authority.

# 6. A minimal set of canonical entities for Nexus v0

If I were bootstrapping this right now, I’d start with a deliberately small inventory ofcanonical topology entitiesand let the model grow.

## 6.1 Core identity / substrate candidates

- Nexus
Nexus

- Nebula
Nebula

- PEB
PEB

- KG(if distinct)
KG(if distinct)

- WRP
WRP

- CIR
CIR

- CER
CER

- CGEL
CGEL

- Conduit
Conduit

- Tackle
Tackle

## 6.2 Likely optional / extracted / capability candidates

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

## 6.3 External / harness / environment entities

- OpenCode
OpenCode

- Ollama
Ollama

- Git
Git

- Docker
Docker

- Portainer
Portainer

## 6.4 Projection / ingestion entities

- DocLing
DocLing

- DocLang
DocLang

- html_importer
html_importer

- GraphBuilder
GraphBuilder

harvested transcript corpus / docs corpus

That’s enough to get a meaningful first self-model without trying to model every package in the repo on day one.

# 7. The report Nexus should be able to generate from this

Once the TSM exists, Nexus should be able to answer a query like:

## “Describe your current topology”

And produce something like:

### A. Core / constitutive components

- Nexus runtime
Nexus runtime

- Nebula
Nebula

- PEB
PEB

- WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL
WRP / CIR / CER / CGEL

- Conduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)
Conduit / Tackle (if those remain constitutive)

### B. Optional capability modules

- LOSM
LOSM

- Vision
Vision

### C. External dependencies / harness concerns

- OpenCode
OpenCode

- Ollama
Ollama

- Git / Docker
Git / Docker

### D. Memory and data flow

- harvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projections
harvested docs → Nebula slices → PEB assertions → topology projections

### E. Known ambiguities

- “LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”
“LOSM classification recently shifted from embedded subsystem toward optional capability module”

- “OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”
“OpenCode mode state is execution metadata only”

### F. Confidence

- “Core identity classifications: high confidence”
“Core identity classifications: high confidence”

- “LOSM boundary: medium confidence”
“LOSM boundary: medium confidence”

- “Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”
“Vision placement: low confidence / not yet stabilized”

That’s the actual practical output of the model.

---

# 8. What I wouldnotdo yet

I would not start by trying to make this a fully normalized universal ontology of every code artifact in Nexus. That will turn into taxonomic sludge.

Instead, I’d start with atwo-speed model:

## Speed 1 — curated semantic topology

A relatively small set of high-valueTopologyEntityrecords:

- systems
systems

- memory substrates
memory substrates

- IRs/contracts
IRs/contracts

- major runtime components
major runtime components

- capability modules
capability modules

- external/harness dependencies
external/harness dependencies

## Speed 2 — harvested structural observations

Repo scans, import graphs, config discoveries, runtime observations that produceTopologyEvidenceand candidate edges/assertions, but donotautomatically become canonical topology truth until promoted.

That matches your existing distinction between:

- what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEB
what we know / canonical semantic state→ PEB

- what we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence
what we’re thinking about / operational working set→ Nebula / harvests / evidence

---

# 9. The one extra object I think you need:TopologyPromotionDecision

Because a lot of this is going to be discovered from scans and harvests, you need a record of when something moves from “candidate topology fact” to “canonical self-model fact.”

```
{  "@type": "TopologyPromotionDecision",  "@id": "promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24",  "candidateAssertion": "assertion:losm-optional-candidate",  "decision": "accepted",  "decidedBy": "Steward",  "decidedAt": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "resultingAssertion": "assertion:losm-optional",  "notes": "LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}
```

```
{  "@type": "TopologyPromotionDecision",  "@id": "promotion:losm-optional-2026-06-24",  "candidateAssertion": "assertion:losm-optional-candidate",  "decision": "accepted",  "decidedBy": "Steward",  "decidedAt": "2026-06-24T23:59:00Z",  "resultingAssertion": "assertion:losm-optional",  "notes": "LOSM is no longer treated as constitutive of Nexus identity."}
```

That gives you a clean governance seam:

- harvests propose
harvests propose

- Steward/Nexus promotes
Steward/Nexus promotes

- PEB holds the canonical assertion
PEB holds the canonical assertion

---

TopologyAssertion

TopologyProjection

TopologyPromotionDecision

EntityKind

IdentityStatus

RelationshipType

AssertionKind

ResponsibilityProfile

RuntimePlacement

ConfidenceScore

# 11. My recommended first-pass workflow

If you want this to become real without boiling the ocean, I’d implement it in this order:

## Step 1 — Curated seed inventory

Manually seed 20–30 high-value entities:

- Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.
Nexus, Nebula, PEB, WRP, CIR, CER, CGEL, Tackle, Conduit, LOSM, Vision, OpenCode, etc.

## Step 2 — Add a small edge vocabulary

Start with:

- contains
contains

- uses
uses

- depends_on
depends_on

- persists_to
persists_to

- projects_to
projects_to

- optional_for
optional_for

- required_for
required_for

- supersedes
supersedes

## Step 3 — Add identity assertions

For each seeded entity, add:

- identityStatus
identityStatus

- responsibilityProfile
responsibilityProfile

- topologyRole
topologyRole

## Step 4 — Wire in evidence harvest

Allow repo scans / docs / GCL / transcripts to produce:

- TopologyEvidence
TopologyEvidence

- candidateTopologyAssertions
candidateTopologyAssertions

- candidateTopologyEdges
candidateTopologyEdges

## Step 5 — Add snapshot generation

Freeze the graph intoTopologySnapshots and generate Nebula review projections.

At that point, Nexus can already begin answering “what do I contain and what do I use?”

----------------------------enumEntityKind{system,module,service,process,runtime,agent,tool,mcp,schema,ir,memory_substrate,capability_module,harness_feature,external_system,ui_surface,database,projection,snapshot,}enumIdentityStatus{constitutive,shared_substrate,optional,external_dependency,experimental,legacy,unknown,}enumRelationshipType{contains,part_of,depends_on,uses,invokes,reads_from,writes_to,persists_to,projects_to,derived_from,supersedes,bridges_to,optional_for,required_for,governed_by,exposed_via,mounted_into,}enumAssertionKind{classification,identity_boundary,dependency_interpretation,topology_claim,responsibility_claim,bridge_decision,deprecation_claim,uncertainty_claim,}enumEvidenceKind{repo_scan,import_graph,runtime_observation,config_observation,document_passage,transcript_quote,change_log_event,steward_annotation,manual_note,}// ----------------------------// CORE STRUCTURES// ----------------------------model TopologySnapshot{snapshotId:string;system:string;capturedAt:utcDateTime;scope:TopologyScope;entityCount:int32;edgeCount:int32;assertionCount:int32;confidenceScore:float32;entities:TopologyEntity[];edges:TopologyEdge[];assertions:TopologyAssertion[];evidence:TopologyEvidence[];projections?:TopologyProjection[];derivedFrom:string[];}model TopologyScope{repos:string[];branches?:string[];workRequests?:string[];harvestWindow?:HarvestWindow;}model HarvestWindow{from:utcDateTime;to:utcDateTime;}// ----------------------------// ENTITY LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyEntity{id:string;name:string;entityKind:EntityKind;identityStatus:IdentityStatus;topologyRole?:string;description?:string;systemAffinity:string[];implementedBy?:string[];consumedVia?:string[];ownedContracts?:string[];runtimePlacement?:RuntimePlacement;tags?:string[];}model RuntimePlacement{placementKind:string;host?:string;external?:boolean;}// ----------------------------// EDGE LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyEdge{id:string;from:string;to:string;relationshipType:RelationshipType;strength?:string;cardinality?:string;semanticMeaning?:string;activationConditions?:string[];evidenceRefs?:string[];}// ----------------------------// ASSERTION LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyAssertion{id:string;subject:string;predicate:string;object:string;assertionKind:AssertionKind;scope:string;confidence:float32;status:string;// provisional | accepted | rejectedrationale?:string;supportedBy?:string[];assertedBy:string;assertedAt:utcDateTime;}// ----------------------------// EVIDENCE LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyEvidence{id:string;evidenceKind:EvidenceKind;capturedAt:utcDateTime;sourceRef:string;summary:string;supports:string[];confidence:float32;}// ----------------------------// PROJECTION LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyProjection{id:string;name:string;projectionKind:string;focusEntities?:string[];focusAssertions?:string[];derivedFromSnapshot:string;}// ----------------------------// PROMOTION / GOVERNANCE// ----------------------------model TopologyPromotionDecision{id:string;candidateAssertion:string;decision:string;// accepted | rejected | deferreddecidedBy:string;decidedAt:utcDateTime;resultingAssertion?:string;notes?:string;}}3. JSON-LD sample instance (LOSM example)This shows how the schema actuallybehavesas a graph.JSON{"@context":"nexus-topology.context.jsonld","@type":"TopologyEntity","entityId":"entity:losm","name":"LOSM","entityKind":"capability_module","identityStatus":"optional","topologyRole":"semantic_execution_engine","systemAffinity": ["Nexus","Assistance"],"description":"Optional semantic execution engine used for graph-based reasoning and process orchestration.","implementedBy": ["repo:losm-kernel","repo:losm-shell","repo:losm-host"],"runtimePlacement": {"placementKind":"external_process","external":true}}4. What this buys you immediatelyThis schema is not just representation — it gives youthree hard capabilities:1. Self-inventoryNexus can answer:what existswhere it liveswhat kind of thing it is2. Self-interpretationBecause of:identityStatusTopologyAssertionTopologyRoleNexus can say:“LOSM is optional”“PEB is constitutive”“OpenCode is external harness state”3. Self-topology explanationBecause of edges + assertions:“what uses what”“why it exists”“what would break if removed”5. The key design win (this is the important part)This schema cleanly separates:Structure (truth)entitiesedgesMeaning (interpretation)assertionsidentityStatustopologyRoleEvidence (epistemology)why we believe anything at allThat separation is what makes:“Nexus can tell us what it is and how it thinks about itself”actually work instead of becoming a static diagram.If you want the next step, the natural continuation is:a query layer for this model(e.g. “show all optional capability modules and what they depend on” or “explain Nexus core identity boundary”), which is where it starts becoming a real introspection engine instead of just a schema.

Good — this is exactly the point where it should stop being conceptual and become something a compiler or generator can actually eat.Below is aconcrete hybrid TypeSpec + JSON-LD framingforNexus.Topology. TopologyEdge{id:string;from:string;to:string;relationshipType:RelationshipType;strength?:string;cardinality?:string;semanticMeaning?:string;activationConditions?:string[];evidenceRefs?:string[];}// TopologyAssertion{id:string;subject:string;predicate:string;object:string;assertionKind:AssertionKind;scope:string;confidence:float32;status:string;// provisional | accepted | rejectedrationale?:string;supportedBy?:string[];assertedBy:string;assertedAt:utcDateTime;}// ----------------------------// EVIDENCE LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyEvidence{id:string;evidenceKind:EvidenceKind;capturedAt:utcDateTime;sourceRef:string;summary:string;supports:string[];confidence:float32;}// ----------------------------// PROJECTION LAYER// ----------------------------model TopologyProjection{id:string;name:string;projectionKind:string;focusEntities?:string[];focusAssertions?:string[];derivedFromSnapshot:string;}// ----------------------------// PROMOTION / GOVERNANCE// ----------------------------model TopologyPromotionDecision{id:string;candidateAssertion:string;decision:string;// accepted | rejected | deferreddecidedBy:string;decidedAt:utcDateTime;resultingAssertion?:string;notes?:string;}}3. Good — this is exactly the point where it should stop being conceptual and become something a compiler or generator can actually eat.

Below is aconcrete hybrid TypeSpec + JSON-LD framingforNexus.Topology. I’m going to structure it so:

- TypeSpec = authoritative schema contract (generation-friendly)
TypeSpec = authoritative schema contract (generation-friendly)

- JSON-LD context = semantic graph binding (self-describing + queryable)
JSON-LD context = semantic graph binding (self-describing + queryable)

- Design stays aligned with your CIR/CER/CGEL/WRP world
Design stays aligned with your CIR/CER/CGEL/WRP world

# 1. JSON-LD Context:nexus-topology.context.jsonld

This defines the semantic graph vocabulary.

```
{  "@context": {    "nexus": "https://nexus.local/ontology/topology#",    "TopologySnapshot": "nexus:TopologySnapshot",    "TopologyEntity": "nexus:TopologyEntity",    "TopologyEdge": "nexus:TopologyEdge",    "TopologyAssertion": "nexus:TopologyAssertion",    "TopologyEvidence": "nexus:TopologyEvidence",    "TopologyProjection": "nexus:TopologyProjection",    "TopologyPromotionDecision": "nexus:TopologyPromotionDecision",    "entityId": "@id",    "type": "@type",    "from": { "@id": "nexus:from", "@type": "@id" },    "to": { "@id": "nexus:to", "@type": "@id" },    "contains": { "@id": "nexus:contains", "@type": "@id" },    "dependsOn": { "@id": "nexus:dependsOn", "@type": "@id" },    "uses": { "@id": "nexus:uses", "@type": "@id" },    "optionalFor": { "@id": "nexus:optionalFor", "@type": "@id" },    "identityStatus": "nexus:identityStatus",    "entityKind": "nexus:entityKind",    "topologyRole": "nexus:topologyRole",    "confidence": "nexus:confidence",    "assertionKind": "nexus:assertionKind",    "evidenceKind": "nexus:evidenceKind",    "capturedAt": {      "@id": "nexus:capturedAt",      "@type": "http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#dateTime"    }  }}
```

{  "@context": {    "nexus": "https://nexus.local/ontology/topology#",    "TopologySnapshot": "nexus:TopologySnapshot",    "TopologyEntity": "nexus:TopologyEntity",    "TopologyEdge": "nexus:TopologyEdge",    "TopologyAssertion": "nexus:TopologyAssertion",    "TopologyEvidence": "nexus:TopologyEvidence",    "TopologyProjection": "nexus:TopologyProjection",    "TopologyPromotionDecision": "nexus:TopologyPromotionDecision",    "entityId": "@id",    "type": "@type",    "from": { "@id": "nexus:from", "@type": "@id" },    "to": { "@id": "nexus:to", "@type": "@id" },    "contains": { "@id": "nexus:contains", "@type": "@id" },    "dependsOn": { "@id": "nexus:dependsOn", "@type": "@id" },    "uses": { "@id": "nexus:uses", "@type": "@id" },    "optionalFor": { "@id": "nexus:optionalFor", "@type": "@id" },    "identityStatus": "nexus:identityStatus",    "entityKind": "nexus:entityKind",    "topologyRole": "nexus:topologyRole",    "confidence": "nexus:confidence",    "assertionKind": "nexus:assertionKind",    "evidenceKind": "nexus:evidenceKind",    "capturedAt": {      "@id": "nexus:capturedAt",      "@type": "http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#dateTime"    }  }}
